[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Design a module named fp_mul_e5f10 that multiplies two floating-point numbers. Format: 16-bit (1 sign + 5 exponent + 10 fraction). Inputs: a and b (16-bit each). Output: y (16-bit). Subnormal numbers must be supported.

Required behaviour, bit-exact: subnormal inputs are used at their true value; results are rounded to nearest, ties to even, including results in the subnormal range; a NaN input and inf x 0 give the canonical quiet NaN 16'h7E00; inf x finite gives inf with the product's sign; an overflowing product gives inf; a zero product gives a signed zero. The testbench enumerates about 18.9 million operand pairs against a reference implementation.

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` in your workspace, a plain readable implementation (unpack, significand multiply, normalise, round, pack) that is correct. Optimize from there.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  localparam BIAS         = 15;
  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  genvar k;
  generate
    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10
  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)
  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];

  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out
  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard
  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule

[Step 2/60]
`timescale 1ns/10ps
// exhaustive-directed tb (fpmul_run)
// Golden-referenced directed testbench: compares the DUT against the embedded
// golden reference (fp_mul_e5f10_ref, generated from context/starting_point.py)
// over ~18.9M enumerated vectors — no stimuli file. Structure:
//   1. the 3 known subnormal-boundary regression pairs (+ golden self-checks)
//   2. every subnormal/zero a (2^11 sign+mantissa patterns) x 4096 structured b
//   3. 4096 structured a x every subnormal/zero b
//   4. 2^21 LFSR-driven broad pairs
// Structured operands: both signs x all 32 exponents x 64 mantissas
// (8 corners + 56 LFSR-derived). The retired file-driven testbench is kept at
// tb_vectors.sv (+ vectors.dat) for cross-checking; it is not compiled.
module tb;
  logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;
  int errors;
  longint total;

  fp_mul_e5f10 dut (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));
  fp_mul_e5f10_ref refm (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y_ref));

  function automatic [15:0] lfsr_next(input [15:0] s);
    lfsr_next = (s >> 1) ^ (s[0] ? 16'hB400 : 16'h0000);
  endfunction

  logic [15:0] structured [0:4095];
  localparam logic [9:0] MANT_CORNERS [0:7] =
      '{10'h000, 10'h001, 10'h002, 10'h003, 10'h3FF, 10'h3FE, 10'h2AA, 10'h155};

  task automatic check();
    #1;
    total = total + 1;
    if (y !== y_ref) begin
      if (errors < 10)
        $display("TB_ERROR a=%0d b=%0d expected_y=%0d actual_y=%0d", a, b, y_ref, y);
      errors = errors + 1;
    end
  endtask

  logic [15:0] lfsr, ra, rb;
  logic [9:0] mant;
  int idx;

  initial begin
    errors = 0; total = 0;

    lfsr = 16'hACE1;
    idx = 0;
    for (int s = 0; s < 2; s++)
      for (int e = 0; e < 32; e++)
        for (int m = 0; m < 64; m++) begin
          if (m < 8) mant = MANT_CORNERS[m];
          else begin lfsr = lfsr_next(lfsr); mant = lfsr[9:0]; end
          structured[idx] = {s[0], e[4:0], mant};
          idx = idx + 1;
        end

    a = 16'h0003; b = 16'h6801; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h0E02) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 1"); end
    a = 16'h8003; b = 16'h6479; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h8AB6) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 2"); end
    a = 16'h7521; b = 16'h0003; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h1BB2) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 3"); end

    for (int sa = 0; sa < 2048; sa++) begin
      a = {sa[10], 5'b00000, sa[9:0]};
      for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin b = structured[i]; check(); end
    end
    for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin
      a = structured[i];
      for (int sb = 0; sb < 2048; sb++) begin b = {sb[10], 5'b00000, sb[9:0]}; check(); end
    end
    ra = 16'h0001; rb = 16'hBEEF;
    for (int k = 0; k < (1 << 21); k++) begin
      ra = lfsr_next(ra); rb = lfsr_next(lfsr_next(rb));
      a = ra; b = rb; check();
    end

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total, errors);
    if (errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

  -> create_file(filename='design.v', content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS         = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n")

[ASSISTANT]
Good, the starting point passes with cost 145429. The delay is 1338ps with target 500ps. Let me analyze the design and think about optimizations.

Key observations:
1. The critical path is very long (1338ps) — goes through the multiply, normalization, rounding, and packing logic
2. The design computes both normal and subnormal paths simultaneously, then muxes
3. The `bit_at` function with variable indexing and the `below` prefix-OR chain are expensive
4. The leading-zero counter is a priority encoder

Let me think about the architecture. The core issue is that the design has two separate normalization/rounding paths (normal and subnormal) both computed in parallel. A cleaner approach would unify these. Also, the dynamic shift amounts and variable indexing create large muxes.

Let me think about a unified approach:
- The product is 22 bits. The exponent sum determines where the result lands.
- We can normalize once (put leading 1 at a fixed position), then shift right for subnormal results, combining the rounding.

Actually, the key insight is: both paths do the same thing — shift the product to a target position and round. The difference is just the shift amount. We can unify by computing a single shift amount that works for both normal and subnormal cases.

Let me think more carefully. In the normal path, we shift so the leading 1 is at bit 10 (of an 11-bit mantissa). In the subnormal path, we shift the product onto a fixed grid (bits 9:0). These are actually the same operation if we think of it as: compute the "true" exponent, determine the shift needed, shift, and round.

Let me try a different approach: compute the exponent, determine if result is normal/subnormal/overflow/underflow, then do a single shift+round. The unified approach would:
1. Compute `exp_sum = ea_eff + eb_eff - 2*BIAS + 1` (the unbiased exponent of the product, since the product of two 1.x significands is 1.xxx, so the result exponent is `ea_eff + eb_eff - BIAS - 1` for the leading-1 position)
2. Actually, let me think about this differently.

The product `prod = m_a * m_b` is a 22-bit number. If both inputs are normal, the product has bits 21:20 as `01` (since both are 11-bit with leading 1, product is ~22 bits with leading 1 at bit 21 or 20). 

Let me think about the unified normalization. The result's mantissa needs the leading 1 at bit 10 (for normal) or the value placed at the subnormal grid. 

A key simplification: instead of finding the leading zero and then doing a bidirectional shift, I can:
- Compute the product
- Find the leading zero count (lz) of the product  
- The normalized product places the leading 1 at a known position
- Then determine the final exponent and whether we need additional right-shifting (for subnormal results)

In the normal path: `exp_result = exp_sum + 1 + carry - (lz - (something))`. The lz accounts for subnormal inputs that have fewer significant bits.

Actually, let me think about this more carefully by understanding the math:

- `m_a` and `m_b` are 11-bit significands (with hidden bit). `prod = m_a * m_b` is up to 22 bits.
- For two normal numbers, `m_a` and `m_b` are in [1024, 2047], so `prod` is in [1048576, 4192256], i.e., 21 or 22 bits. Leading 1 is at bit 21 or 20.
- For subnormal inputs, the leading 1 could be at any lower position.

The current code normalizes by putting the leading 1 at bit 10, giving an 11-bit mantissa. The exponent is then `exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz + BIAS` ... actually the code does it through `limit_under` and `limit_over`.

Let me try a different, simpler unified approach. I'll compute:
- `prod` (22-bit)
- `lz` = leading zero count of prod (position of MSB from the left)
- After normalization, the leading 1 is at bit 10. The "normalized exponent" is `exp_sum + 1 - lz + 21` ... hmm, this is getting complex.

Let me instead focus on reducing area since the delay is already heavily violated (1338 vs 500 target), meaning the tool is already maximizing speed. Let me try increasing target_delay to let the tool optimize for area instead.

[ASSISTANT]
OK so with target_delay=300, we get the same result as default (500). The tool is already at maximum effort for speed. The cost is 145429 with area 108.7 and delay 1338.

Now let me focus on structural optimizations to reduce both area and delay. The key issues:
1. Two parallel normalization+rounding paths (normal and subnormal) — lots of duplicated logic
2. The `bit_at` function with variable indexing creates large muxes
3. The `below` prefix-OR chain (22 bits)
4. The leading-zero priority encoder
5. Dynamic shifters

Let me think about a unified approach. The core idea: instead of having separate normal and subnormal paths, I can:
1. Normalize the product (put leading 1 at a fixed position) 
2. Compute the "raw" exponent 
3. If the result would be subnormal (exponent too small), do additional right-shifting from the normalized position
4. Round once

This way there's one shift+round path instead of two.

Let me think through the math carefully:

- `prod = m_a * m_b` (22 bits, `PROD_W=22`)
- `lz` = number of leading zeros in prod (0 means MSB at bit 21)
- After normalizing (shifting so leading 1 is at bit 10): the shift is `21 - lz - 10 = 11 - lz` to the right (if lz <= 11), or `lz - 11` to the left (if lz > 11).
- The "normalized exponent" before bias adjustment: `exp_sum + 1 + carry` where `exp_sum = ea_eff + eb_eff`. But we need to account for lz: the leading-zero count reduces the exponent.

Actually, looking at the code more carefully:
- `limit_under = BIAS + lz` — this is the threshold: if `lhs <= limit_under`, the result is subnormal.
- `lhs = exp_sum + 1 + carry`
- `e_norm = lhs - limit_under` (the biased exponent for normal results)

So the "true" exponent of the result (biased) is `lhs - lz` (since lz effectively means the product has fewer significant bits, reducing the exponent by lz). Wait, let me think again...

The product `m_a * m_b` where both are `1.x` (11-bit) normally gives a 22-bit result with the leading bit at 21 or 20. The biased exponent of the product (if both were normal with exponents ea, eb) would be `ea + eb - BIAS + 1` (if leading bit at 21) or `ea + eb - BIAS` (if leading bit at 20, i.e., product is 21 bits wide).

With subnormals, the leading zero count adjusts this. The code's `lhs = exp_sum + 1 + carry` and then `limit_under = BIAS + lz`. So the normal biased exponent is `lhs - limit_under + BIAS = exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz`. And the unbiased exponent would be `exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz - BIAS = (ea_eff - BIAS) + (eb_eff - BIAS) + 1 + carry - lz`. This makes sense: the product of two `1.xxx` significands is `1.xxx * 2^(ea_eff-15) * 1.xxx * 2^(eb_eff-15) = (product) * 2^(ea_eff+eb_eff-30)`. The product has its leading bit at position `21 - lz`, so the value is `prod * 2^(ea_eff + eb_eff - 30 - 21 + lz)`... hmm this is getting complicated.

Let me take a different approach. Instead of trying to deeply understand the math, let me focus on **merging the two paths** into one. The key insight: both paths compute a shift amount, shift the product, extract guard/sticky bits, and round. If I can compute a single unified shift and round, I save a lot of logic.

Actually, let me think about this more concretely. The normal path:
- Normalizes to put leading 1 at bit 10 → shift by `sr = 11 - lz` (right) or `sl = lz - 11` (left)
- Result mantissa is bits [10:0] of the shifted value
- Guard/sticky come from bits below
- After rounding, if carry, exponent increases by 1

The subnormal path:
- Shifts by `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` (right shift to put result on the subnormal grid)
- Result fraction is bits [9:0]
- Guard/sticky from below
- After rounding, if carry, the result becomes the smallest normal (exp=1)

These are actually the same operation if I think of it as: "shift the product so that the result's binary point is at a fixed position, then extract the fraction and round." The difference is just the shift amount.

For a normal result: the shift puts the leading 1 at bit 10 (after considering lz and carry).
For a subnormal result: the shift puts the product on the 10-bit subnormal grid.

The key realization: I can compute a single shift amount that works for both. Let me define:
- `shift = 11 - lz` for the normal case (right shift, possibly negative = left shift)
- For subnormal: `shift = 26 - exp_sum` (right shift)

Actually these are related. In the normal case, `shift = 11 - lz`, and the normal exponent is `exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz - 15` (biased). For subnormal, we need more right-shifting.

Let me think about it as: after normalizing (leading 1 at bit 10), the biased exponent is `e = exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz`. If `e <= 0` (i.e., subnormal range), we need to shift right by `1 - e` more bits. If `e > 0`, it's normal.

This is the unified approach! Let me implement it:

1. Normalize: shift product so leading 1 is at bit 10. This gives `mant_pre[10:0]`, guard, sticky, round, carry.
2. Compute `e_raw = exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz` (biased exponent of normalized result, where `e_raw = 1` means smallest normal).
3. If `e_raw >= 1`: normal result with exponent `e_raw` and fraction from `mant_post`.
4. If `e_raw <= 0`: subnormal result — shift right by `1 - e_raw` additional bits. This means we need to re-extract the fraction and re-round.

Hmm, but this still has two rounding paths (the normal round and the subnormal re-shift+round). The issue is that the carry from the first rounding changes the shift needed for subnormal, creating a circular dependency.

Actually, in the original code, the subnormal path doesn't use the normalized result at all — it goes back to the raw `prod` and shifts it by `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`. And the normal path uses the carry. So there's no circular dependency.

Let me think about whether I can merge them. The subnormal path's `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` and the normal path's shift `sr = 11 - lz` (right) are related: `total_shift = sr + (15 + 1 - lz) = sr + (16 - lz)`. Hmm, and `exp_sum = ea_eff + eb_eff`, while `lz` depends on the product. For normal inputs, `lz` is 0 or 1, and `exp_sum = ea + eb`. For subnormal inputs, both `lz` and `exp_sum` change.

Actually wait — `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum = (15 + 10 + 1) - exp_sum = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - exp_sum`. And the normal path shift is `sr = 11 - lz`. The relationship: `total_shift = sr + (15 + 1 - lz - exp_sum + exp_sum + lz)`... I'm going in circles.

Let me take a step back. Rather than trying to perfectly merge the paths, let me try simpler optimizations first:

1. **Replace the `bit_at` function with direct indexing** — the function creates synthesis overhead
2. **Simplify the `below` computation** — use a simpler sticky computation
3. **Reduce the LZC** — use a simpler structure
4. **Merge the two rounding paths** if possible

Actually, let me try a more radical rewrite. Let me think about the whole design from scratch with a unified shift-and-round approach.

The idea: 
- Compute `prod` (22 bits)
- Compute `exp_raw = ea_eff + eb_eff - 2*BIAS + 1` (unbiased exponent, assuming product is normalized to `1.xxx`)
- But we need to account for subnormal inputs reducing the effective magnitude
- Actually, the LZC already accounts for this

Let me try yet another approach. Instead of the leading-zero-based normalization, I'll:
1. Compute the product
2. Compute the "effective exponent" `e = ea_eff + eb_eff - 2*BIAS` (unbiased)
3. The product value is `prod * 2^e * 2^(-21)` (since prod is 22 bits, the MSB represents 2^21 * 2^e)
   Wait, let me be careful. `m_a` is an 11-bit integer with value `1.xxx * 2^10` or `0.xxx * 2^10` (for subnormals). The actual significand value is `m_a / 2^10` (since the hidden bit is at position 10). So `prod = m_a * m_b` has value `(m_a/2^10) * (m_b/2^10) * 2^20 = prod_val / 2^20`. And the actual value is `prod_val / 2^20 * 2^(ea_eff - 15) * 2^(eb_eff - 15) = prod_val * 2^(ea_eff + eb_eff - 30 - 20) = prod_val * 2^(ea_eff + eb_eff - 50)`.

The result should be `1.xxx * 2^(result_exp)` where `result_exp = ea_eff + eb_eff - 15` (for the case where both are normal and product has leading bit at 21, i.e., `prod_val >= 2^21`). In this case `prod_val * 2^(ea_eff + eb_eff - 50) = (prod_val / 2^21) * 2^(ea_eff + eb_eff - 50 + 21) = (prod_val / 2^21) * 2^(ea_eff + eb_eff - 29)`. Since `prod_val / 2^21` is `1.xxx`, the unbiased exponent is `ea_eff + eb_eff - 29 = ea_eff + eb_eff - 2*15 + 1`. Good.

If the product has its leading bit at position 20 (`prod_val < 2^21`), the exponent is one less: `ea_eff + eb_eff - 30`.

In general, with leading bit at position `21 - lz`, the unbiased exponent is `ea_eff + eb_eff - 50 + 21 - lz = ea_eff + eb_eff - 29 - lz`.

The biased exponent is then `ea_eff + eb_eff - 29 - lz + 15 = ea_eff + eb_eff - 14 - lz`. And the code has `lhs = exp_sum + 1 + carry = ea_eff + eb_eff + 1 + carry`, and `limit_under = 15 + lz`, so `e_norm = lhs - limit_under = ea_eff + eb_eff + 1 + carry - 15 - lz`. For carry=0, this is `ea_eff + eb_eff - 14 - lz`. Matches!

OK so the biased exponent of the normalized result is `e_biased = ea_eff + eb_eff - 14 - lz + carry`.

For a normal result: `1 <= e_biased <= 30` (biased exponent 1 to 30).
For subnormal: `e_biased <= 0`, meaning we need to shift right by `1 - e_biased` extra bits.
For overflow: `e_biased > 30`.

Now the unified approach:
1. Normalize the product: shift so leading 1 is at bit 10 → `mant_norm[10:0]` (11 bits), plus guard and sticky.
2. Round: `mant_round = mant_norm + round_bit`. This may produce carry.
3. Compute `e_biased = ea_eff + eb_eff - 14 - lz + carry_round`.
4. If `e_biased >= 1` and `e_biased <= 30`: normal result.
5. If `e_biased <= 0`: subnormal — need to shift the *rounded* result right by `1 - e_biased` bits. But rounding changes the mantissa, which changes what we shift... This is the circular dependency issue.

The problem is that subnormal results need to be rounded *after* the final shift, not before. The original code handles this by having a separate path that shifts the raw product and then rounds.

So the two paths are fundamentally necessary because rounding happens at different points. However, we can still try to share the shift logic or simplify other parts.

Let me focus on practical improvements instead:

1. **Replace `bit_at` function** with explicit wire computations
2. **Simplify the `below` sticky computation** 
3. **Simplify the LZC** using a more compact form
4. **Combine the two shift+round paths** to share the shifter

Let me try a rewrite that keeps the same algorithm but is more synthesizer-friendly.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier\n// Unified normalization + rounding to reduce area and delay.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n\n  // unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15], s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10], e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0], f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0), e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31), e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);\n  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & ~|f_a, zero_b = e_b_zero & ~|f_b;\n  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones &  |f_a, nan_b  = e_b_ones &  |f_b;\n  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & ~|f_a, inf_b  = e_b_ones & ~|f_b;\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [5:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 6'd1 : {1'b0, e_a};\n  wire [5:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 6'd1 : {1'b0, e_b};\n\n  // significand product (22 bits)\n  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count of prod (priority encoder)\n  wire [4:0] lz;\n  wire [21:0] prod_rev = {<<{prod}}; // bit-reversed\n  // lz = number of leading zeros = position of MSB from top\n  // Using OR-reduction of upper bits\n  wire or21 = prod[21];\n  wire or20 = |prod[21:20];\n  wire or19 = |prod[21:19];\n  wire or18 = |prod[21:18];\n  wire or17 = |prod[21:17];\n  wire or16 = |prod[21:16];\n  wire or15 = |prod[21:15];\n  wire or14 = |prod[21:14];\n  wire or13 = |prod[21:13];\n  wire or12 = |prod[21:12];\n  wire or11 = |prod[21:11];\n  wire or10 = |prod[21:10];\n  wire or9  = |prod[21:9];\n  wire or8  = |prod[21:8];\n  wire or7  = |prod[21:7];\n  wire or6  = |prod[21:6];\n  wire or5  = |prod[21:5];\n  wire or4  = |prod[21:4];\n  wire or3  = |prod[21:3];\n  wire or2  = |prod[21:2];\n  wire or1  = |prod[21:1];\n  wire or0  = |prod[21:0];\n\n  assign lz = ~or21 ? 5'd21 :\n              ~or20 ? 5'd20 :\n              ~or19 ? 5'd19 :\n              ~or18 ? 5'd18 :\n              ~or17 ? 5'd17 :\n              ~or16 ? 5'd16 :\n              ~or15 ? 5'd15 :\n              ~or14 ? 5'd14 :\n              ~or13 ? 5'd13 :\n              ~or12 ? 5'd12 :\n              ~or11 ? 5'd11 :\n              ~or10 ? 5'd10 :\n              ~or9  ? 5'd9 :\n              ~or8  ? 5'd8 :\n              ~or7  ? 5'd7 :\n              ~or6  ? 5'd6 :\n              ~or5  ? 5'd5 :\n              ~or4  ? 5'd4 :\n              ~or3  ? 5'd3 :\n              ~or2  ? 5'd2 :\n              ~or1  ? 5'd1 : 5'd0;\n\n  // sticky: below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR)\n  wire [21:0] below;\n  assign below[0] = prod[0];\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 1; k < 22; k = k + 1)\n      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];\n  endgenerate\n\n  // helper: bit at position idx in prod (0 if out of range)\n  // For the normal path: guard at (sr-1), sticky at (sr-2) where sr = 11 - lz\n  // For the subnormal path: guard at (total_shift-1), sticky at (total_shift-2)\n\n  // === NORMAL PATH ===\n  // normalize: leading 1 at bit 10\n  wire need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0] sr = 5'd11 - lz;  // right shift amount (0..11)\n  wire [4:0] sl = lz - 5'd11;  // left shift amount (0..10)\n\n  wire [21:0] shifted_r = prod >> sr;\n  wire [21:0] shifted_l = prod << sl;\n  wire [21:0] shifted = need_right ? shifted_r : shifted_l;\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre = shifted[10:0];\n\n  // guard and sticky for normal path\n  wire [4:0] gr_idx = sr - 5'd1;  // guard bit position = sr-1\n  wire guard_n    = need_right & (sr >= 5'd1) & prod[gr_idx];\n  wire sticky_n   = need_right & (sr >= 5'd2) & below[gr_idx - 5'd1];\n\n  wire round_n  = guard_n & (sticky_n | mant_pre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] mant_round_n = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};\n  wire        carry_n = mant_round_n[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post_n = carry_n ? mant_round_n[11:1] : mant_round_n[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post_n[9:0];\n\n  // exponent for normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum = {2'd0, ea_eff} + {2'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry_n};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = 8'd15 + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = 8'd45 + {3'd0, lz};  // 15 + 30 + lz\n  wire underflow = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire overflow  = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm_8 = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm = e_norm_8[4:0];\n\n  // === SUBNORMAL PATH ===\n  wire [7:0] total_shift_8 = 8'd26 - exp_sum; // 15 + 10 + 1 - exp_sum\n  wire [4:0] total_shift = total_shift_8[4:0];\n  wire ts_valid = (total_shift_8[7] == 1'b0) && (total_shift < 5'd22);\n  wire [21:0] sub_shifted = ts_valid ? // fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier
// Unified normalization + rounding to reduce area and delay.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);

  localparam BIAS = 15;

  // unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15], s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10], e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0], f_b = b[9:0];

  // classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0), e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == 5'd31), e_b_ones = (e_b == 5'd31);
  wire zero_a = e_a_zero & ~|f_a, zero_b = e_b_zero & ~|f_b;
  wire nan_a  = e_a_ones &  |f_a, nan_b  = e_b_ones &  |f_b;
  wire inf_a  = e_a_ones & ~|f_a, inf_b  = e_b_ones & ~|f_b;

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // effective significands and exponents
  wire [10:0] m_a = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [5:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 6'd1 : {1'b0, e_a};
  wire [5:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 6'd1 : {1'b0, e_b};

  // significand product (22 bits)
  wire [21:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  // leading-zero count of prod (priority encoder)
  wire [4:0] lz;
  // lz = number of leading zeros = position of MSB from top
  // Using OR-reduction of upper bits
  wire or21 = prod[21];
  wire or20 = |prod[21:20];
  wire or19 = |prod[21:19];
  wire or18 = |prod[21:18];
  wire or17 = |prod[21:17];
  wire or16 = |prod[21:16];
  wire or15 = |prod[21:15];
  wire or14 = |prod[21:14];
  wire or13 = |prod[21:13];
  wire or12 = |prod[21:12];
  wire or11 = |prod[21:11];
  wire or10 = |prod[21:10];
  wire or9  = |prod[21:9];
  wire or8  = |prod[21:8];
  wire or7  = |prod[21:7];
  wire or6  = |prod[21:6];
  wire or5  = |prod[21:5];
  wire or4  = |prod[21:4];
  wire or3  = |prod[21:3];
  wire or2  = |prod[21:2];
  wire or1  = |prod[21:1];
  wire or0  = |prod[21:0];

  assign lz = ~or21 ? 5'd21 :
              ~or20 ? 5'd20 :
              ~or19 ? 5'd19 :
              ~or18 ? 5'd18 :
              ~or17 ? 5'd17 :
              ~or16 ? 5'd16 :
              ~or15 ? 5'd15 :
              ~or14 ? 5'd14 :
              ~or13 ? 5'd13 :
              ~or12 ? 5'd12 :
              ~or11 ? 5'd11 :
              ~or10 ? 5'd10 :
              ~or9  ? 5'd9 :
              ~or8  ? 5'd8 :
              ~or7  ? 5'd7 :
              ~or6  ? 5'd6 :
              ~or5  ? 5'd5 :
              ~or4  ? 5'd4 :
              ~or3  ? 5'd3 :
              ~or2  ? 5'd2 :
              ~or1  ? 5'd1 : 5'd0;

  // sticky: below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR)
  wire [21:0] below;
  assign below[0] = prod[0];
  generate
    for (genvar k = 1; k < 22; k = k + 1)
      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];
  endgenerate

  // helper: bit at position idx in prod (0 if out of range)
  // For the normal path: guard at (sr-1), sticky at (sr-2) where sr = 11 - lz
  // For the subnormal path: guard at (total_shift-1), sticky at (total_shift-2)

  // === NORMAL PATH ===
  // normalize: leading 1 at bit 10
  wire need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0] sr = 5'd11 - lz;  // right shift amount (0..11)
  wire [4:0] sl = lz - 5'd11;  // left shift amount (0..10)

  wire [21:0] shifted_r = prod >> sr;
  wire [21:0] shifted_l = prod << sl;
  wire [21:0] shifted = need_right ? shifted_r : shifted_l;
  wire [10:0] mant_pre = shifted[10:0];

  // guard and sticky for normal path
  // guard at prod[sr-1], sticky at below[sr-2]
  // sr ranges 0..11 when need_right
  wire guard_n  = need_right & (sr >= 5'd1) & prod[sr - 5'd1];
  wire sticky_n = need_right & (sr >= 5'd2) & below[sr - 5'd2];

  wire round_n  = guard_n & (sticky_n | mant_pre[0]);
  wire [11:0] mant_round_n = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_n};
  wire        carry_n = mant_round_n[11];
  wire [10:0] mant_post_n = carry_n ? mant_round_n[11:1] : mant_round_n[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post_n[9:0];

  // exponent for normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum = {2'd0, ea_eff} + {2'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry_n};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = 8'd15 + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = 8'd45 + {3'd0, lz};  // 15 + 30 + lz
  wire underflow = (lhs <= limit_under);
  wire overflow  = (lhs >  limit_over);
  wire [7:0] e_norm_8 = lhs - limit_under;

  wire [4:0] total_shift = total_shift_8[4:0];
  wire ts_valid = (total_shift_8[7] == 1'b0) && (total_shift < 5'd22);
  wire [21:0] sub_shifted = ts_valid ? (prod >> total_shift) : 22'd0;
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire [4:0] gs_idx = total_shift - 5'd1;
  wire guard_s  = ts_valid & (total_shift >= 5'd1) & prod[gs_idx];
  wire sticky_s = ts_valid & (total_shift >= 5'd2) & below[total_shift - 5'd2];
  wire round_s  = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s = frac_sum[10];

`v20 = |prod[21:20]` — are bits 21 or 20 set? If yes, the MSB is at 20 or 21. Since we already know bit 21 is not set (v21=0), if v20=1 then MSB is at 20, lz=1.

Wait no! The priority encoder should give `lz` = number of leading zeros = 21 - (position of highest set bit). So:
- If v21 (= prod[21]) is 1, lz = 0.
- Else if v20 (= |prod[21:20]) is 1, lz = 1. (MSB at 20)
- Else if v19 (= |prod[21:19]) is 1, lz = 2. (MSB at 19)
- ...

So the check should be `v21 ? 0 : v20 ? 1 : v19 ? 2 : ...` or equivalently `~v21 ? (v20 ? 1 : v19 ? 2 : ...) : 0`.

But in my code, I wrote `~v21 ? 5'd21 :` which gives lz=21 when v21=0. That's backwards! It should be `v21 ? 5'd0 : ~v20 ? 5'd1 : ~v19 ? 5'd2 : ...`

The original code's loop does: `if (prod[i]) lz = 21 - i`. This finds the highest i where prod[i] is 1, and sets lz = 21 - i. The last iteration where prod[i]=1 wins (since we iterate i from 0 to 21, the highest i wins).

So lz = 21 - (highest set bit position). For prod=3075, highest set bit = 11, lz = 21 - 11 = 10.

My priority encoder is wrong. Let me fix it:

- If prod[21]=1, lz=0
- Else if prod[20]=1, lz=1
- Else if prod[19]=1, lz=2
- ...

Using OR-reductions of the upper bits:
- `v21 = prod[21]` → if v21, lz=0
- `v20 = |prod[21:20]` → if v20 (and not v21), MSB at 20, lz=1
- `v19 = |prod[21:19]` → if v19 (and not v20), MSB at 19, lz=2
- ...

So: `lz = v21 ? 0 : v20 ? 1 : v19 ? 2 : ... : v0 ? 21 : 21`

Or equivalently: `lz = ~v21 ? (~v20 ? (~v19 ? ... : 2) : 1) : 0` — no, this is the same issue.

The correct form with OR-reductions checking from MSB down:
`lz = ~or_top(21) ? ~or_top(20) ? ... : 1 : 0`

Wait, let me think about this differently. Define `p[k] = |prod[21:k]` = OR of bits 21 down to k. Then:
- If `p[21]` = prod[21] is 1, lz=0
- If `p[20]` = |prod[21:20] is 1 but p[21]=0... no, if p[21]=0 then p[20]=prod[20]. This is getting confusing.

Let me just use: `p[k] = |prod[21:k]`. Then `lz = 21 - k` where k is the largest k such that `p[k] = 1`. Since `p[k] = p[k+1] | prod[k]`, we have `p[k] >= p[k+1]`. The largest k with `p[k]=1` is the position of the MSB.

The priority encoder: `lz = p21 ? 0 : p20 ? 1 : p19 ? 2 : ... : p0 ? 21 : 21`

where `p21 = prod[21]`, `p20 = |prod[21:20]`, `p19 = |prod[21:19]`, etc.

But `p20 >= p21` always, so if p21=1 then p20=1 too. The priority encoder checks p21 first, then p20, etc. If p21=1, lz=0. If p21=0 and p20=1, then MSB is at 20, lz=1. If p21=0 and p20=0 and p19=1, MSB at 19, lz=2. Etc.

So: `lz = p21 ? 0 : p20 ? 1 : p19 ? 2 : ... : p0 ? 21 : 21` (if all 0, lz=21 meaning prod=0).

But my code has it backwards with `~v21 ? 21 : ~v20 ? 20 : ...` which is wrong. Let me fix this.

Let me start from the original `design.v` and try merging the two paths. The idea: both paths shift `prod` right and extract bits. The normal path shifts by `sr = 11 - lz` (0..11), the subnormal path shifts by `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` (could be larger). Actually, the real opportunity is: both paths compute guard and sticky from the same `prod` and `below` signals. And both compute a rounded result. If I can merge the shifter, I save area.

But the shift amounts come from different computations. Let me think about whether I can unify the shift.

For the normal path: `sr = 11 - lz` (right shift, 0..11), or `sl = lz - 11` (left shift, 0..10).
For the subnormal path: `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` (right shift, 0..21 typically).

When underflow (subnormal case): the result uses `total_shift`. When not underflow (normal case): the result uses `sr` or `sl`.

But `underflow` depends on `carry`, which depends on the normal path rounding, which depends on the normal path shift... So there's a dependency chain. The original computes both in parallel, then muxes. Merging them would require computing the shift amount before knowing whether it's underflow.

Actually, I can compute: `final_shift = underflow ? total_shift : (need_right ? sr : -sl)`. But `underflow` depends on `carry` which depends on the normal rounding. So I'd need to compute the normal rounding first, then decide the shift. This serializes the computation and would increase delay.

Let me try a different approach. Instead of tweaking incrementally, let me think about the fundamental architecture.

The biggest area/delay cost components:
1. The 11×11 multiplier (22-bit product) — this is fixed, can't reduce
2. The LZC (priority encoder, 22 levels)
3. Two barrel shifters (normal: bidirectional, subnormal: right only)
4. Two rounding adders
5. The `below` prefix-OR chain
6. The `bit_at` function calls (variable indexing → muxes)
7. Exponent computation and comparison
8. Final output muxing

The biggest lever might be to **merge the two paths**. Let me think about this more carefully.

Actually, looking at the timing path, it goes through: classification → multiplier → LZC → shift → round → exponent → pack. The delay is dominated by the long combinational chain.

A key insight: for the **normal case** (which is the common case), the LZC is typically 0 or 1 (both inputs normal, product is 21-22 bits). The subnormal path is only needed when the result is in the subnormal range. 

What if I restructure to compute the result in a unified way? Here's an idea:

1. Compute `prod` and `exp_sum`
2. Compute `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` (the shift to place the result on the subnormal grid)
3. If `total_shift < 0`: the result is normal/overflow, normalize by LZC
4. If `total_shift >= 0` and `total_shift < 22`: the result is subnormal, shift right by `total_shift`
5. If `total_shift >= 22`: the result might be subnormal-just-rounding-to-1 or zero

But the issue is that the LZC-based normalization and the total_shift-based subnormal shift are fundamentally different operations. The LZC normalizes the product to have its leading bit at position 10. The total_shift places it on a fixed grid.

Actually, I realize there IS a way to unify them. The key: the subnormal path's `total_shift` and the normal path's `sr` are related. In the normal path, `sr = 11 - lz`, and the normalized mantissa has its leading 1 at bit 10. The normal exponent is `e = exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz - 15` (biased). If `e <= 0` (subnormal), we need to shift right by `1 - e = lz + 15 - exp_sum - carry` more bits from the normalized position. The total shift from the raw product is `sr + (1 - e) = (11 - lz) + (lz + 15 - exp_sum - carry) = 26 - exp_sum - carry`. Which is `total_shift - carry`! 

So `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` and the actual subnormal shift is `total_shift - carry`. But `carry` depends on the normal rounding, which is a problem...

Actually wait — the original subnormal path does NOT use the carry from the normal path. It computes `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` (without carry) and shifts the *raw* product. So the subnormal shift is `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`, independent of carry. The carry from the subnormal path's own rounding (`carry_s`) determines if the result rounds up to the smallest normal.

So the two paths are truly independent (both start from `prod`). The only shared signals are `prod`, `below`, and `exp_sum`. Merging the shifters doesn't make sense because the shift amounts are different and both are needed.

Let me try a completely different strategy: **simplify the design by eliminating the LZC and the bidirectional shifter**. 

Instead of normalizing by LZC, I can use the exponent to determine the shift. The product has its MSB at position 21 or lower. The "normalized exponent" is `exp_sum + 1 - lz`. But lz = 21 - (position of MSB). So `exp_sum + 1 - lz = exp_sum + 1 - 21 + MSB_pos = exp_sum - 20 + MSB_pos`.

Hmm, this still requires finding the MSB position. 

Let me try another approach: **always right-shift the product to a fixed position and then handle the exponent**. The idea:
- The product is 22 bits. The maximum product (2047 * 2047 = 4190209) has MSB at bit 21.
- Always normalize assuming MSB at bit 21 (shift right by 11 to get 11-bit mantissa with leading 1 at bit 10)
- Then adjust for the actual MSB position

Actually, let me try a simpler optimization first. Let me go back to the original `design.v` and just try to optimize the synthesis by restructuring the LZC into a tree and simplifying the `below` computation. Also, let me try replacing the `bit_at` function with a generate-based approach.

Let me try a more structural approach: compute `guard` and `sticky` bits using explicit shifted versions of `prod` instead of variable indexing.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier\n// Based on starting_point.v with structural optimizations.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count using a balanced tree\n  // Group into 4-bit blocks, find first non-zero block\n  function [3:0] lzc4;\n    input [3:0] v;\n    begin\n      lzc4 = ~v[3] ? (~v[2] ? (~v[1] ? 4'd3 : 4'd2) : 4'd1) : 4'd0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire [3:0] lz_b0 = lzc4(prod[3:0]);\n  wire [3:0] lz_b1 = lzc4(prod[7:4]);\n  wire [3:0] lz_b2 = lzc4(prod[11:8]);\n  wire [3:0] lz_b3 = lzc4(prod[15:12]);\n  wire [3:0] lz_b4 = lzc4(prod[21:16]); // 6-bit block\n\n  wire nz_b0 = |prod[3:0];\n  wire nz_b1 = |prod[7:4];\n  wire nz_b2 = |prod[11:8];\n  wire nz_b3 = |prod[15:12];\n  wire nz_b4 = |prod[21:16];\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  always @* begin\n    if (nz_b4)      lz = {1'b0, lz_b4};\n    else if (nz_b3) lz = 5'd4 + {1'b0, lz_b3};\n    else if (nz_b2) lz = 5'd8 + {1'b0, lz_b2};\n    else if (nz_b1) lz = 5'd12 + {1'b0, lz_b1};\n    else if (nz_b0) lz = 5'd16 + {1'b0, lz_b0};\n    else            lz = 5'd22; // all zero\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR) -- only need specific indices\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  assign below[0] = prod[0];\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 1; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1)\n      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];\n  endgenerate\n\n  // bit_at: v[idx], 0 outside the vector\n  function bit_at;\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. normalise and round a normal result\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? The LZC is wrong. The `lzc4` function and block grouping is incorrect. Let me check: for `prod = 3075` (a=3, b=26625 case), `lz_b4 = lzc4(prod[21:16])`. prod = 3075 = `00 0000 0000 1011 1010 0011` (22 bits). `prod[21:16]` = bits 21-16 = all 0. `lzc4(0000) = ~0 ? (~0 ? (~0 ? 3 : 2) : 1) : 0` = 3. `nz_b4 = |0000 = 0`. So we go to b3. `prod[15:12]` = bits 15-12 = `0000` (3075 < 4096). `nz_b3 = 0`. b2: `prod[11:8]` = bits 11-8 = `1011` = 0xB = 11. `nz_b2 = 1`. `lz_b2 = lzc4(1011)`. `lzc4(1011) = ~v[3]=0 ? ... : 0` = 0 (since v[3]=1). `lz = 8 + 0 = 8`. But the correct lz is 10! The MSB of 3075 is at bit 11, so lz = 21 - 11 = 10.

The problem is my grouping. Bits 11-8 = `1011`, and the MSB of this group is at bit 3 of the group = bit 11 overall. So the position within the 4-bit block is 3 (MSB first). The LZC of the block is 0 (leading zero count within the block, considering MSB first). So the overall position = block_offset + block_lzc = 8 + 0 = 8. But the actual MSB is at bit 11, not bit 8. The issue is that lz should be 21 - 11 = 10, and 21 - (8 + 3) = 10. So I need `lz = 21 - (block_base + (3 - block_lzc))`.

Actually, `lzc4` gives the number of leading zeros *within the block* (from the MSB of the block). For `1011`, leading zeros = 0 (MSB is 1). So the MSB position within the block is 3 (from LSB). Overall MSB position = block_base + 3 - lzc4 = 8 + 3 - 0 = 11. And lz = 21 - 11 = 10. But my code computes `lz = 8 + lz_b2 = 8 + 0 = 8`. That's wrong!

The issue is that `lz = 21 - msb_position`, and `msb_position = block_base + (3 - lzc4)`. So `lz = 21 - block_base - 3 + lzc4 = 18 - block_base + lzc4`. For block 2 (base 8): `lz = 18 - 8 + lzc4 = 10 + lzc4`. For `1011`, `lzc4 = 0`, so `lz = 10`. Correct!

But my code does `lz = block_base + lzc4` which is wrong. Let me fix the formula. Actually, it's easier to just revert to the original LZC approach which is known to work, and focus on other optimizations.

OK v3 is now identical to the original. Let me now focus on a fundamentally different approach to reduce area. Let me think about what the main area consumers are.

The design has:
1. 11×11 multiplier → ~200+ gates equivalent
2. Two barrel shifters (normal: bidirectional 0-11, subnormal: right 0-21)
3. LZC priority encoder
4. `below` prefix-OR (22 gates)
5. `bit_at` variable indexing (muxes)
6. Two rounding adders
7. Exponent arithmetic and comparisons
8. Output muxes

The multiplier is fixed. Let me focus on reducing the auxiliary logic. Key ideas:

**Idea 1: Unify the shifter.** Both paths shift `prod` right. The normal path shifts by `sr = 11 - lz` (right) or `sl = lz - 11` (left). The subnormal path shifts by `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` (right). 

If I make the normalizer always shift right (pad the product to handle left shifts), I can use a single barrel shifter. For left shift by `sl`, that's equivalent to right shift by a negative amount, which I can represent as a larger right shift of a wider (padded) product.

Actually, let me think about this differently. Instead of two separate shifters, I can compute the "final shift amount" that selects between normal and subnormal:
- If underflow: shift = `total_shift` 
- If not underflow: shift = `sr` (or `-sl` for left)

But underflow depends on carry which depends on the shift... circular.

**Idea 2: Eliminate the subnormal path entirely by extending the normal path.** The normal path normalizes to mantissa at bits [10:0]. If the result is subnormal, we need additional right-shifting. Instead of a separate path, I can:
1. Normalize (normal path)
2. Compute the "extra shift" = `1 - e_biased` if e_biased <= 0
3. Right-shift the rounded result by the extra shift, re-round

But re-rounding after the first round is incorrect (double rounding). The original avoids this by shifting the raw product and rounding once.

**Idea 3: Reduce the `below` prefix-OR.** The `below` array is used by `bit_at(below, sr-2)` and `bit_at(below, total_shift-2)`. Since `sr` ranges 0..11 and `total_shift` ranges 0..21, we need `below[0..20]`. But we compute all 22 bits. We can't easily reduce this since the index is variable.

**Idea 4: Replace `bit_at` with a barrel shifter for guard/sticky.** Instead of variable indexing, use a shift to extract the guard and sticky bits. For the normal path, `guard = (prod >> (sr-1))[0]` and `sticky = |(prod >> (sr-1+1))`. But this requires another shifter.

Let me try a different strategy: **merge the guard/sticky computation into the barrel shifter**. When we do `shifted = prod >> sr`, the bit that "falls off" the bottom is the guard bit, and the OR of all bits below that is the sticky. So instead of separately computing guard/sticky, I can extract them from the shift operation.

For a barrel shifter that does `prod >> sr`, the output bits [10:0] are the mantissa, bit [11] (conceptually) would be the guard, and the OR of bits below the guard is the sticky. If I extend the shifter to output a few extra bits, I get guard and sticky for free.

Let me try this approach:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier\n// Unifies normal and subnormal shift+round into one path using a single\n// barrel shifter that produces mantissa + guard + sticky.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v3.v')

That's just the original again. Let me think more carefully about what to actually optimize.

Let me try a completely different approach: **always compute the subnormal path, and derive the normal path from it**. 

The key insight: the subnormal path computes `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` and shifts the product right. The normal path shifts by `sr = 11 - lz`. When the result is normal, `total_shift < 0` (the result's unbiased exponent is positive), so the subnormal path gives 0. When the result is subnormal, `total_shift >= 0` and the subnormal path gives the right answer.

For the normal case, instead of the LZC-based normalization, I can use the fact that for normal results, the product's MSB is at bit 21 (lz=0) or bit 20 (lz=1). Actually, lz can be 0 to 11 for normal results (need_right is true when lz <= 11).

Hmm, this doesn't simplify much. Let me try a different approach.

**Alternative: reduce the `below` prefix-OR and `bit_at` calls by computing guard/sticky from the shifted output.**

When we compute `shifted = prod >> sr`, the `shifted` value contains the mantissa in bits [10:0], and bits [11] and beyond would contain the guard. But we also need the sticky (OR of all bits below guard). 

If I extend the shifter to produce bits [12:0] (mantissa + guard), I get the guard for free. The sticky is the OR of all bits below the guard, which is `below[sr-2]` = `|(prod >> sr)[below guard]`... this is still complex.

Actually, let me try a simpler optimization: **share the `below` array between the two paths** (which the original already does) and **try to reduce the number of `bit_at` calls**.

Looking at the original, there are 4 `bit_at` calls:
1. `bit_at(prod, sr - 1)` — normal guard
2. `bit_at(below, sr - 2)` — normal sticky
3. `bit_at(prod, total_shift - 1)` — subnormal guard
4. `bit_at(below, total_shift - 2)` — subnormal sticky

Each `bit_at` with a variable index creates a 22:1 mux (selecting one of 22 bits based on a 5-bit index). That's 4 large muxes.

Can I replace the variable indexing with a shifter? `bit_at(prod, idx)` = `(prod >> idx)[0]`. And `bit_at(below, idx)` = `(below >> idx)[0]`. So I could compute `(prod >> (sr-1))[0]` and `(below >> (sr-2))[0]` using shifters. But shifters with variable shift amounts are also expensive...

Actually, the shifters are already computed! `shifted = prod >> sr`. So `shifted[0]` is the bit just below the mantissa LSB, and `shifted[-1]` (conceptually) would be the guard. Actually `shifted = prod >> sr`, so `shifted[0] = prod[sr]`, `shifted[1] = prod[sr+1]`, etc. And the guard is `prod[sr-1]`. The sticky is `below[sr-2] = |prod[sr-2:0] = |(prod >> (sr-1))[below]`...

If I compute `prod >> sr`, the bits that are shifted out are `prod[sr-1:0]`. The guard is `prod[sr-1]` (the MSB of the shifted-out part), and the sticky is `|prod[sr-2:0]` (the OR of the rest). So I can compute:
- `shifted_out = prod[sr-1:0]` (but sr is variable, so this is still a variable slice)
- guard = MSB of shifted_out
- sticky = OR of shifted_out without MSB

This is still variable indexing.

For subnormal inputs, the MSB could be lower, but subnormal results are handled by the subnormal path anyway.

**Alternative: normalize by shifting the product left to align the MSB at bit 21, then shift right by 11.** This is equivalent to: `mantissa = (prod << lz) >> 11` = `prod << (lz - 11)` = `prod >> (11 - lz)` for lz <= 11. Same as the original's right shift.
