[ASSISTANT]
The synthesis tool gives the same result (area=108.7, delay=1338) regardless of target_delay (500, 800, 1200 all give the same). It seems the tool is at its maximum effort for this design structure. The cost is fixed at 145429.

Let me now focus on structural changes. The key idea I want to try: **merge the normal and subnormal paths by using a single shift+round computation**.

Here's the unified approach I'll try:

1. Compute `prod` and `exp_sum`
2. Compute `lz` (leading zero count)
3. **Unify the shift:** Define `shift_amount = max(sr_normal, total_shift_sub)` where we use the subnormal shift when underflow and normal shift otherwise. But this has the circular dependency on `carry`.

Actually, let me try a completely different formulation. Instead of normalizing by LZC, I'll:
1. Always right-shift `prod` by a single amount that places the result correctly
2. The shift amount combines the normalization shift and the subnormal adjustment

The key: the product `prod` has MSB at position `21 - lz`. The result needs:
- For normal: leading 1 at bit 10, so shift right by `11 - lz` (if lz <= 11) or left by `lz - 11` (if lz > 11)
- For subnormal: shift right by `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`

The relationship: `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum` and the normal right shift `sr = 11 - lz`. When the result is subnormal (exp_sum is small), `total_shift` is large. When normal, `total_shift` is small or negative.

The normal exponent (biased) is `e = exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz - 15`. For normal results, `e >= 1`, so `exp_sum + carry - lz >= 15`. For subnormal, `e <= 0`, so `exp_sum + carry - lz < 15`.

But `carry` depends on rounding which depends on the shift... This is the fundamental circular dependency. The original breaks it by computing both paths in parallel.

Let me try a different tactic: **reduce the `below` prefix-OR to only the bits actually needed, and compute guard/sticky from the shifted value directly.**

For the normal path, when `need_right` and `sr` right shift: `shifted = prod >> sr`. The guard is `prod[sr-1]` and sticky is `|prod[sr-2:0]`. If I compute the shifted value with one extra bit below, I get guard = `shifted_ext[0]` where `shifted_ext = prod >> (sr - 1)` for sr >= 1. But this is yet another variable shift.

OK, let me try a completely different approach. Let me compute a **sticky bit that covers all cases** and see if I can simplify.

Actually, let me try the most radical simplification: **compute the product, then always shift it to a fixed position (bit 21 as MSB, normalized to bit 10 as mantissa with the understanding that lz adjusts the exponent), and then handle subnormal results by additional shifting from the normalized position.**

Wait, I keep going in circles. Let me try something concrete: **reduce the number of `bit_at` calls by computing guard and sticky from the barrel shifter output.**

When `shifted = prod >> sr`, the "lost" bits are `prod[sr-1:0]`. If I also compute `lost = prod & ((1 << sr) - 1)`, then guard = `lost[sr-1]` and sticky = `|lost[sr-2:0]`. But computing `lost` requires masking with a variable mask, which is as expensive as `bit_at`.

Let me try yet another approach: **pre-shift the product to a fixed position and then use fixed-position guard/sticky.**

For the normal path, instead of variable-shifting, I can:
1. Left-shift prod by `lz` to align MSB at bit 21 → `prod_aligned = prod << lz`
2. Right-shift by 11 → `mantissa = prod_aligned >> 11 = prod << (lz - 11)` if lz >= 11, or `prod >> (11 - lz)` if lz < 11
3. Guard = `prod_aligned[10]` (fixed position!), sticky = `|prod_aligned[9:0]` (fixed position!)

Wait, this is interesting! If I left-shift prod by lz first (aligning MSB to bit 21), then the mantissa is bits [21:11] of the aligned product, the guard is bit 10, and the sticky is `|bits[9:0]`. All at fixed positions!

Let me verify: if prod has MSB at position `21 - lz`, then `prod << lz` has MSB at position 21. The mantissa (leading 1 at bit 10 of the result) is bits [21:11] = 11 bits. The guard is bit 10. The sticky is `|bits[9:0]`.

But wait, the original normalizes to bit 10, meaning the leading 1 is at bit 10 of an 11-bit value. That's `mant_pre[10] = 1`, and `frac_norm = mant_pre[9:0]`. If I align MSB to bit 21, then `mant_pre = prod_aligned[21:11]` (11 bits), guard = `prod_aligned[10]`, sticky = `|prod_aligned[9:0]`. This is exactly equivalent to the original's approach but with fixed-position guard/sticky!

And for the left-shift case (lz > 11, need_right = false): `sl = lz - 11`, `shifted = prod << sl`. The mantissa is `shifted[10:0]`. With the aligned approach: `prod_aligned = prod << lz`, mantissa = `prod_aligned[21:11]`, guard = `prod_aligned[10]`, sticky = `|prod_aligned[9:0]`. When lz > 11, `prod << lz` might overflow 22 bits! If prod has MSB at position `21 - lz` where lz > 11, then MSB position < 10. `prod << lz` would put MSB at position 21, but the product is only 22 bits, so bits below position 21 - lz would be shifted in as zeros. Wait, but `prod << lz` for a 22-bit value with lz up to 21: if lz = 21 (prod = 0), `prod << 21 = 0`. If lz = 12 (MSB at bit 9), `prod << 12` shifts MSB to bit 21, and bits 0-8 become 0. The mantissa is bits [21:11] which would be `prod[9:0] << 10`... Hmm, this might not work because the product is only 22 bits and shifting left by lz could lose bits.

Actually wait, if prod has MSB at position `21 - lz`, then `prod << lz` moves the MSB to position 21. The lower `21 - lz` significant bits move up too. Since the original product is at most 22 bits and lz <= 21, `prod << lz` fits in... well, if lz = 0, no shift, MSB at 21, all 22 bits fit. If lz = 10, MSB at 11, `prod << 10` moves MSB to 21, and the lowest bit moves to bit 10. The product's bits 0-10 (11 bits) move to positions 10-20. So `prod << 10` has bits in positions 10-21 (12 bits). The mantissa is [21:11] = 11 bits, guard = bit 10, sticky = |bits[9:0] = 0 (since the shifted-in zeros are at positions 0-9). This seems correct!

For lz = 12 (MSB at bit 9, need_right = false): `prod << 12` moves MSB to bit 21. Original bits 0-9 (10 bits) move to positions 12-21. Bits 0-11 are 0. Mantissa = [21:11] = 11 bits. But the original product's bits 0-9 are only 10 bits, so the mantissa would be {prod[9:0], 0} = 11 bits with the MSB at bit 21 = prod[9]. Wait, `prod << 12`: bit 21 = prod[9] (MSB), bit 20 = prod[8], ..., bit 12 = prod[0], bits 11:0 = 0. So mantissa [21:11] = {prod[9:0], 0}. The leading 1 is at bit 21 (if prod[9] = 1). frac = {prod[8:0], 0} = prod[8:0] << 1.

In the original approach: `sl = lz - 11 = 1`. `shifted = prod << 1`. `mant_pre = shifted[10:0] = (prod << 1)[10:0]`. If prod's MSB is at bit 9, `prod << 1` has MSB at bit 10. `mant_pre[10] = prod[9]` = 1. `mant_pre[9:0] = {prod[8:0], prod[0] shifted...}`. Actually `mant_pre = (prod << 1)[10:0]`. If prod has bits at positions 0-9, then `prod << 1` has bits at 1-10. `mant_pre[10:0] = {prod[9], prod[8], ..., prod[0], 0}`. So `mant_pre = {prod[9:0], 1'b0}`.

In the aligned approach: `prod << lz = prod << 12`. mantissa [21:11] = `{prod[9], prod[8], ..., prod[0], 1'b0}` = same as `mant_pre` above! And guard = `prod_aligned[10] = 0` (since bit 10 of `prod << 12` is 0). And sticky = `|prod_aligned[9:0] = 0`. In the original: guard = 0, sticky = 0 (since `need_right = false`). Match!

So the unified approach works: **left-shift prod by lz, then extract mantissa from fixed positions [21:11], guard from [10], sticky from |[9:0].** This eliminates the bidirectional shifter (always left-shift) and the variable `bit_at` calls for guard/sticky (fixed positions).

But I still need a variable left-shift by `lz` (0-21). And I need the subnormal path separately. However, the subnormal path can also use a similar approach — but it needs a right-shift by `total_shift`, which is different.

Actually, can I unify even further? The subnormal path shifts `prod` right by `total_shift` and extracts frac from [9:0]. With the aligned approach, `prod_aligned = prod << lz`. The subnormal result is `(prod >> total_shift)[9:0]`. Can I express this in terms of `prod_aligned`?

`prod >> total_shift = (prod_aligned >> lz) >> total_shift = prod_aligned >> (lz + total_shift)`. And `lz + total_shift = lz + 26 - exp_sum`. The fraction is `(prod_aligned >> (lz + 26 - exp_sum))[9:0]`.

Hmm, this introduces a new variable shift. But the shift amount is `lz + 26 - exp_sum` which could be large. This doesn't simplify.

Let me at least try the normal path optimization (fixed-position guard/sticky via left-shift) and keep the subnormal path as-is. This should reduce the variable indexing in the normal path.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier\n// Normal path: left-shift prod by lz to align MSB at bit 21, then extract\n// mantissa/guard/sticky from fixed positions (eliminates bit_at calls).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count (same as original)\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR) -- only needed for subnormal path\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. NORMAL PATH: left-shift prod by lz to align MSB at bit 21\n  //    Then mantissa = prod_aligned[21:11], guard = prod_aligned[10],\n  //    sticky = |prod_aligned[9:0]\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = prod_aligned[21:11];\n  wire              guard      = prod_aligned[10];\n  wire              sticky     = |prod_aligned[9:0];\n  wire              round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v4.v')

For subnormal, the shift is `lz + 26 - exp_sum`. If the result is normal, `exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz > 15`, i.e., `exp_sum - lz > 14`, so `lz + 26 - exp_sum < 12`. Let me try computing the subnormal guard/sticky using the same approach as the normal path but with a different shift. Specifically: for the subnormal path, I'll shift `prod_aligned` right by `lz + total_shift` and extract from fixed positions.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier\n// Both paths use prod_aligned = prod << lz, with fixed-position guard/sticky.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // Normalize: left-shift prod by lz, MSB at bit 21\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;\n\n  // below_aligned[k] = |prod_aligned[k:0] (prefix OR for sticky in sub path)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below_a;\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below_a[k] = |prod_aligned[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // 5. NORMAL PATH: fixed-position extraction\n  wire [10:0] mant_pre  = prod_aligned[21:11];\n  wire        guard     = prod_aligned[10];\n  wire        sticky    = |prod_aligned[9:0];\n  wire        round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. SUBNORMAL PATH: shift prod_aligned right by (lz + total_shift)\n  //    total_shift = 26 - exp_sum, so sub_shift = lz + 26 - exp_sum\n  wire [7:0] sub_shift_8 = {3'd0, lz} + 8'd26 - exp_sum;\n  wire [4:0] sub_shift   = sub_shift_8[4:0];\n  wire       sub_valid   = (sub_shift_8[7] == 1'b0) && (sub_shift < 5'd22);\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] sub_shifted = sub_valid ? (prod_aligned >> sub_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n\n  // guard/sticky for subnormal: at fixed positions of (prod_aligned >> sub_shift)\n  // guard = prod_aligned[sub_shift - 1], sticky = |prod_aligned[sub_shift - 2:0]\n  // Use bit_at-style function on prod_aligned\n  function bit_at_a;\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input [4:0] idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at_a = (idx <= 5'd21) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire guard_s    = sub_valid ? bit_at_a(prod_aligned, sub_shift - 5'd1) : 1'b0;\n  wire sticky_s   = sub_valid ? bit_at_a(below_a, sub_shift - 5'd2) : 1'b0;\n  wire round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v5.v')

// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier
// Normal path: left-shift prod by lz to align MSB at bit 21, then extract
// mantissa/guard/sticky from fixed positions (eliminates bit_at calls).

module fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);

  localparam BIAS = 15;
  localparam MAX_E = 31;
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W = 22;

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  // leading-zero count (same as original)
  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR) -- only needed for subnormal path
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  generate
    for (genvar k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. NORMAL PATH: left-shift prod by lz to align MSB at bit 21
  //    Then mantissa = prod_aligned[21:11], guard = prod_aligned[10],
  //    sticky = |prod_aligned[9:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = prod_aligned[21:11];
  wire              guard      = prod_aligned[10];
  wire              sticky     = |prod_aligned[9:0];
  wire              round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule


[ASSISTANT]
Good. Let me now try to optimize the v4 design. Key ideas:

1. **Simplify the `below` prefix-OR**: The `below` array is used by `bit_at(below, total_shift - 2)`. `total_shift` ranges from -∞ to 26. When valid (0-22), we need `below[0..20]`. But `below[21]` is never needed (total_shift max is 22, so total_shift-2 = 20). So I can reduce to 21 bits.

2. **Try computing the subnormal sticky differently**: Instead of `bit_at(below, total_shift-2)`, which requires the full `below` array and variable indexing, compute the sticky from the shifted output. When `sub_shifted = prod >> total_shift`, the bits that fall off are `prod[total_shift-1:0]`. The guard is `prod[total_shift-1]` and the sticky is `|prod[total_shift-2:0]` = `below[total_shift-2]`. But I already have `sub_shifted` — can I extract guard/sticky from extra bits of the shifter?

If I compute `prod >> (total_shift - 1)` instead of `prod >> total_shift`, I get the guard at bit 0 and the mantissa at bits [1:10] (for 10-bit fraction). The sticky would be `|(prod >> total_shift)[below]`... this is getting circular.

Actually, let me try a different approach for the subnormal path: extend the shifter to output more bits. Instead of `sub_shifted[9:0]`, compute `sub_shifted[11:0]` where bit 0 is the guard and the sticky is computed from a separate OR reduction.

Let me try: `sub_ext = prod >> (total_shift - 1)` when `total_shift >= 1`. Then:
- `frac_trunc = sub_ext[10:1]` (the 10-bit fraction)
- `guard_s = sub_ext[0]` (the guard bit)
- `sticky_s = |(prod >> total_shift)[below]`... no, this is still variable.

Actually, the sticky is `|prod[total_shift-2:0]`. This is the OR of all bits below the guard. If I compute `sub_sticky = prod >> total_shift`, then the shifted-out bits are `prod[total_shift-1:0]`. The guard is `prod[total_shift-1]` and the sticky is `|prod[total_shift-2:0]`. I can compute `prod_and_below = prod >> (total_shift - 1)` to get guard at bit 0, and the sticky is `|prod_and_below` for bits below... no, `prod_and_below = prod >> (total_shift - 1)` has guard at bit 0, and bits below are 0 (already shifted out).

Hmm, the issue is that `prod >> (total_shift - 1)` loses the bits below the guard. I need those bits for sticky. 

Let me try a different approach: compute the sticky as `(prod << (32 - total_shift)) != 0`... but this requires 32-bit shifts.

Actually, the simplest approach: compute `sticky_any = |(prod << (PROD_W - total_shift + 1))` which shifts the bits below the guard to the MSB and ORs them. But this is still a variable shift.

Let me try yet another approach: **use `prod >> total_shift` and check if the remainder is nonzero**. The remainder of `prod >> total_shift` is `prod & ((1 << total_shift) - 1)`. The guard is the MSB of the remainder, and the sticky is the OR of the rest. But computing the remainder with a variable mask is as expensive as the prefix-OR.

OK, let me try a practical optimization: **remove `below[21]` since it's never needed, and simplify the `bit_at` function to use a 5-bit parameter instead of integer**. Also, let me try to reduce the subnormal path by combining the guard/sticky computation.

Actually, let me try a more impactful change: **eliminate the `bit_at` function entirely for the subnormal path by using a single barrel shifter that produces guard + sticky**. 

The idea: compute `sub_ext = {1'b0, prod} >> total_shift` (23-bit). Then:
- `frac_trunc = sub_ext[9:0]`
- `guard_s = sub_ext[10]` (bit just below the fraction)
- `sticky_s = |sub_ext[22:11]`... no, this is the bits ABOVE the fraction, not below.

Hmm, right-shifting moves bits DOWN. So `prod >> total_shift` puts the fraction at bits [9:0], and the guard at bit [10] (if total_shift >= 1, the guard is `prod[total_shift-1]` which maps to bit 0 of the remainder). Actually:

`prod >> total_shift`: bit 0 of the result = `prod[total_shift]`, bit 9 = `prod[total_shift+9]`. The guard is `prod[total_shift-1]` which is NOT in the result (it's been shifted out). So I can't get the guard from the shifted result.

But if I shift by `total_shift - 1` instead: `(prod >> (total_shift - 1))`. Bit 0 = `prod[total_shift-1]` = guard. Bits [10:1] = fraction. But the sticky (bits below guard) = `|prod[total_shift-2:0]` = `|(prod >> total_shift)[below]`... I need to compute this separately.

Actually, there's a clever trick: **compute the sticky by OR-reducing the shifted-out bits**. If I compute `shifted_out = prod & ((1 << total_shift) - 1)`, then guard = `shifted_out[total_shift-1]` and sticky = `|shifted_out[total_shift-2:0]`. But computing `shifted_out` with a variable mask is expensive.

Let me try a completely different approach: **compute guard and sticky using a single right-shift of a wider value**. Pad `prod` with zeros on the left, shift right by `total_shift`, and look at specific positions:

`prod_wide = {22'd0, prod}` (44-bit). `prod_wide >> total_shift`. Then bit `total_shift` is at position... this doesn't help.

OK, let me try something simpler. Let me just **remove the `bit_at` function and use explicit conditional logic**. The `bit_at` function with `integer` parameter may be inhibiting Yosys optimization. Let me replace it with explicit wire logic using 5-bit indexing.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier v6\n// Normal path: left-shift prod by lz, fixed-position extraction.\n// Subnormal path: explicit guard/sticky without bit_at function.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR) -- for subnormal sticky\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  assign below[0] = prod[0];\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 1; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1)\n      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];\n  endgenerate\n\n  // 5. NORMAL PATH: left-shift prod by lz to align MSB at bit 21\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = prod_aligned[21:11];\n  wire              guard      = prod_aligned[10];\n  wire              sticky     = |prod_aligned[9:0];\n  wire              round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. SUBNORMAL PATH\n  // total_shift = 26 - exp_sum (right shift of prod to subnormal grid)\n  // Use 6-bit to handle range -4..26\n  wire [6:0] ts7 = 7'd26 - {1'b0, exp_sum};  // 26 - exp_sum (7-bit, can be negative via wrap)\n  wire       ts_neg = exp_sum > 7'd26;  // total_shift would be negative\n  wire [4:0] total_shift = ts7[4:0];\n  wire       ts_ge0      = ~ts_neg;\n  wire       ts_lt22     = ts_ge0 & (total_shift < 5'd22);\n\n  // Subnormal fraction: prod >> total_shift\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] sub_shifted = ts_lt22 ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n\n  // Guard and sticky using explicit indexing with range checks\n  // guard = prod[total_shift - 1] if total_shift >= 1 and total_shift <= 21\n  // sticky = below[total_shift - 2] if total_shift >= 2 and total_shift <= 21\n  // For total_shift = 0: no guard (shift by 0, nothing lost)\n  // For total_shift = 1: guard = prod[0], sticky = 0\n  // For total_shift >= 22: guard = prod[21] if total_shift = 22, else 0\n\n  // Use a 32-bit extended prod/below to avoid x from out-of-bounds indexing\n  wire [31:0] prod_ext  = prod;\n  wire [31:0] below_ext = below;\n  wire [4:0]  ts_m1     = total_shift - 5'd1;\n  wire [4:0]  ts_m2     = total_shift - 5'd2;\n\n  wire guard_s  = ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 5'd1) & prod_ext[ts_m1];\n  wire sticky_s = ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 5'd2) & below_ext[ts_m2];\n\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v6.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Wait, but when `total_shift = 22`, the guard at position 21 is valid (`prod[21]`), but my condition `total_shift <= 5'd21` would reject it. In the original, `bit_at(prod, 21)` returns `prod[21]` because `21 < 22`. So `total_shift = 22` should give guard = `prod[21]`. Let me fix: the guard is valid when `total_shift - 1 <= 21`, i.e., `total_shift <= 22`. Similarly, sticky is valid when `total_shift - 2 <= 21`, i.e., `total_shift <= 23`.

But `prod` is 22 bits (indices 0-21). `prod[ts_m1]` where `ts_m1 = total_shift - 1`. For `total_shift = 22`, `ts_m1 = 21`, which is valid. For `total_shift = 23`, `ts_m1 = 22`, which is out of range. So the condition should be `ts_m1 <= 21` or equivalently `total_shift <= 22`.

But `total_shift` is 5-bit and can be up to 26. When `total_shift = 22`, `ts_m1 = 21` (valid). When `total_shift = 23`, `ts_m1 = 22` (out of range for 22-bit prod). When `total_shift = 26`, `ts_m1 = 25` (out of range). And when `total_shift` wraps (exp_sum > 26), `total_shift` could be anything.

Also, when `ts_neg` (exp_sum > 26), `ts_ge0 = 0`, so guard_s = 0. That's correct.

Let me fix the condition:

Good, v6 is back to 131196. Now let me try to optimize the LZC. The current `for` loop in the `always` block creates a priority encoder that Yosys synthesizes. Let me try a tree-based approach that might be more efficient.

Looking at the timing path, it starts at `a[14]` and goes through the classifier, then through the multiplier, LZC, shifter, rounder, exponent, and packer. The LZC doesn't seem to be on the critical path directly (the path goes through `a[14]` which is the exponent, not the fraction/multiply path). The critical path seems to be: exponent → classification → exp_sum → total_shift → sub_shift → guard/sticky → round → frac_sub → pack.

Let me look at the path more carefully. It starts at `a[14]` (exponent bit) and ends at `y[4]` (fraction bit). The path goes through XNOR/XOR gates (likely the multiplier or exponent comparison), then through the O2A1O1I gates (likely the barrel shifter or adder), then through the AOI gates (likely the packing mux).

Let me try a different optimization: **simplify the exponent comparison logic**. The `underflow` and `overflow` comparisons use 8-bit comparators. Let me try to simplify them.

Also, let me try to **reduce the `below` prefix-OR** from 22 to 21 bits (since `below[21]` = `|prod[21:0]` = `|prod` which is only needed when `total_shift = 23`, but in that case `sticky = below[21]` which would be 1 if prod is nonzero. But `total_shift = 23` means `exp_sum = 3`, and the guard is at position 22 (out of range), so guard_s = 0 and sticky_s doesn't matter).

Actually, let me look at what indices `below` is accessed at: `below_ext[ts_m2]` where `ts_m2 = total_shift - 2`. `total_shift` ranges 0..26 (when ts_ge0). So `ts_m2` ranges from -2 (wrapped to 30) to 24. But when `total_shift >= 2`, `ts_m2` ranges 0..24. With 32-bit `below_ext`, indices 0..24 are valid (bits 22-31 are 0). So `below[22..24]` are accessed but are always 0 (since `below` is 22-bit, extended to 32 with zeros). This is correct.

Let me try a different approach: **reduce the exponent width**. The `exp_sum` is 8-bit, but `ea_eff` and `eb_eff` are each 5-bit (0-31), so `exp_sum` is at most 62 (6-bit). Using 6-bit for `exp_sum` might reduce the comparator sizes.

Also, let me try to **combine the `underflow` and `overflow` checks** more efficiently.

Let me try making these changes:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier v7\n// Narrower exponent arithmetic, simplified comparisons.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR) -- for subnormal sticky\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  assign below[0] = prod[0];\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 1; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1)\n      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];\n  endgenerate\n\n  // 5. NORMAL PATH: left-shift prod by lz to align MSB at bit 21\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = prod_aligned[21:11];\n  wire              guard      = prod_aligned[10];\n  wire              sticky     = |prod_aligned[9:0];\n  wire              round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result (use 6-bit for exp_sum since max is 62)\n  wire [5:0] exp_sum_6  = ea_eff + eb_eff;  // max 31+31 = 62\n  wire [6:0] lhs_7      = {1'b0, exp_sum_6} + 7'd1 + {6'd0, carry};\n  wire [6:0] limit_u_7  = 7'd15 + {2'd0, lz};\n  wire [6:0] limit_o_7 = 7'd45 + {2'd0, lz};  // 15 + 30 + lz\n  wire       underflow = (lhs_7 <= limit_u_7);\n  wire       overflow  = (lhs_7 >  limit_o_7);\n  wire [6:0] e_norm_7  = lhs_7 - limit_u_7;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm   = e_norm_7[4:0];\n\n  // 7. SUBNORMAL PATH\n  // total_shift = 26 - exp_sum\n  wire [6:0] ts7       = 7'd26 - {1'b0, exp_sum_6};\n  wire       ts_neg    = (exp_sum_6 > 6'd26);\n  wire [4:0] total_shift = ts7[4:0];\n  wire       ts_ge0    = ~ts_neg;\n  wire       ts_lt22   = ts_ge0 & (total_shift < 5'd22);\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] sub_shifted = ts_lt22 ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n\n  wire [31:0] prod_ext  = prod;\n  wire [31:0] below_ext = below;\n  wire [4:0]  ts_m1     = total_shift - 5'd1;\n  wire [4:0]  ts_m2     = total_shift - 5'd2;\n\n  wire guard_s  = ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 5'd1) & prod_ext[ts_m1];\n  wire sticky_s = ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 5'd2) & below_ext[ts_m2];\n\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v7.v')

[ASSISTANT]
V8 (with 6-bit exp_sum) is the same as v7 (139558) and worse than v6. The 6-bit exp_sum doesn't help — it actually increases delay because the narrower arithmetic creates a different (longer) critical path. V6 with 8-bit exp_sum remains the best at 131196.

Let me go back to v6 and try different optimizations. Let me look at what's on the critical path of v6 more carefully. The path starts at `a[14]` and ends at `y[4]`. It goes through:
- Classification (NOR5, OAI21, NAND4, AND2)
- XNOR/XOR chain (multiplier output processing)
- A2O1A1O1I (adder/shifter)
- O2A1O1I (adder)
- Various AOI/OAI (comparisons/muxes)
- Final output muxing

The critical path seems to be through the subnormal path (exponent → total_shift → guard/sticky → round → frac_sub → pack). Let me try to speed up the subnormal path.

One idea: **compute the subnormal guard/sticky from a single barrel shift of prod**. Instead of separately computing `prod_ext[ts_m1]` and `below_ext[ts_m2]`, I can shift `prod` right by `total_shift - 1` and extract guard from bit 0, and sticky from the OR of the remaining bits.

But the sticky would be `|prod[total_shift-2:0]` which requires a variable OR. The `below` array already computes this.

Let me try another approach: **precompute the sticky for all possible shift amounts and select**. This is a mux-based approach. For `total_shift` in 0..22, the sticky is `below[total_shift - 2]`. I could build a 22:1 mux for the sticky. But that's what the variable indexing already does.

Let me try yet another idea: **simplify the `below` prefix-OR into a tree structure** that might be faster.

Actually, let me try a more radical approach: **eliminate the `below` array entirely and compute the sticky from the shifted output**. When I compute `sub_shifted = prod >> total_shift`, the bits that are shifted out are `prod[total_shift-1:0]`. The guard is the MSB of these shifted-out bits, and the sticky is the OR of the rest. 

If I compute `sub_guard_sticky = prod >> (total_shift - 1)` (shifting by one less), then:
- bit 0 = guard = prod[total_shift - 1]
- The sticky is `|prod[total_shift-2:0]` = `|(sub_guard_sticky >> 1) & mask`... this doesn't simplify.

Actually, there's a neat trick: if I compute `prod >> (total_shift - 1)` into a wide enough result, then:
- The result's bit 0 is the guard
- The result's bits [1:...] are the mantissa and above
- The sticky is the OR of bits below the guard in the ORIGINAL prod

The sticky is NOT available from the shifted result (those bits are lost). But I can compute the sticky separately as `|(prod & ((1 << (total_shift-1)) - 1))`. This requires a variable mask AND a reduction OR, which is also expensive.

Let me try a completely different approach: **compute the subnormal result by extending the normal path**. The idea:

1. Normalize prod (left-shift by lz, MSB at bit 21)
2. Round the normal mantissa → `mant_round`, `carry`
3. Compute `e_biased = exp_sum + 1 + carry - lz - BIAS` (biased exponent of normalized result)
4. If `e_biased >= 1`: normal result
5. If `e_biased <= 0`: subnormal — shift `mant_round` right by `1 - e_biased` bits, re-round

The problem with this approach is **double rounding**: the normal path rounds, then the subnormal path shifts and rounds again. This can give different results than rounding once.

BUT — there's a way to avoid double rounding: keep the guard and sticky from the normalization, and when shifting right for subnormal, combine the old guard/sticky with the newly shifted-out bits.

Actually, the original code avoids this by having a separate path from the raw product. Let me think about whether I can use the normalized product (before rounding) for the subnormal path.

The normalized product is `prod_aligned = prod << lz` (MSB at bit 21). The subnormal path needs `prod >> total_shift = prod_aligned >> (lz + total_shift)`. The fraction is `(prod_aligned >> (lz + total_shift))[9:0]`. 

Now, `lz + total_shift = lz + 26 - exp_sum`. Let me call this `sub_shift_final`. The fraction is `(prod_aligned >> sub_shift_final)[9:0]`, the guard is `prod_aligned[sub_shift_final - 1]`, and the sticky is `|prod_aligned[sub_shift_final - 2:0]`.

This is the same as what v5 tried, and it was worse because the additional `below_a` array and the `sub_shift_final` computation added more logic. But the key difference is that `prod_aligned` is already computed for the normal path. If I can share the barrel shifter between the two paths...

Actually, let me try to **share the barrel shifter**. Both paths need to shift `prod` or `prod_aligned`:
- Normal: `prod << lz` (left shift by 0-21)
- Subnormal: `prod >> total_shift` (right shift by 0-22)

If I make one bidirectional shifter: `shifted = prod << lz` for normal, `prod >> total_shift` for subnormal. The shift direction and amount are different, so I'd need a mux on the shift direction and a combined shift amount. This doesn't simplify since the shift amounts are computed independently.

Let me try a different optimization: **simplify the classification logic**. The `nan_a`, `nan_b`, `inf_a`, `inf_b`, `zero_a`, `zero_b` signals each check specific conditions. Let me see if I can simplify them.

Also, let me try to **reduce the `below` prefix-OR to only 21 bits** (since `below[21]` is only used when `total_shift = 23`, but `total_shift = 23` means `ts_m2 = 21` which is valid for a 22-bit `below`). So I can't reduce below 22 bits. But actually, `below` is accessed via `below_ext[ts_m2]` where `below_ext` is 32-bit. When `ts_m2 >= 22`, `below_ext[ts_m2]` accesses bits 22-31 which are 0 (from the 32-bit zero-extension). So `below` only needs 22 bits, which it already has.

Let me try a different approach entirely: **compute the sticky using a different method**. Instead of the prefix-OR `below`, I can compute the sticky as `|(prod >> total_shift)` for the subnormal path, where the shifted result's lower bits contain the shifted-out data. Wait, `prod >> total_shift` shifts RIGHT, so the shifted-out bits (lower bits) are lost. The sticky is the OR of the bits that were at positions below the guard. 

Hmm, let me think about this differently. For the subnormal path:
- `sub_shifted = prod >> total_shift` → fraction is `sub_shifted[9:0]`
- The guard is `prod[total_shift - 1]` (the bit just below the fraction)
- The sticky is `|prod[total_shift - 2:0]` (all bits below the guard)

I can compute this as: `lost_bits = prod - (sub_shifted << total_shift)` (the remainder). Then `guard = lost_bits[total_shift - 1]` and `sticky = |lost_bits[total_shift - 2:0]`. But computing `lost_bits` requires a shift and subtract, which is expensive.

Let me try another idea: **compute the guard and sticky together as a single 32-bit OR-reduction**. 

`guard_sticky = |(prod << (32 - total_shift))` gives the OR of all bits from position `total_shift - 1` down to 0 (conceptually). But this requires a 32-bit left shift, which is expensive.

OK, let me try something more practical. Let me try to **reduce the size of the `prod_ext`/`below_ext` from 32 to 22 bits** by using a different approach for the out-of-range case. The issue was that 22-bit indexing with a 5-bit index that exceeds 21 produces 'x' in simulation. Let me use a generate-based mux instead:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier v9\n// Based on v6 with mux-based guard/sticky extraction for subnormal path.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  assign below[0] = prod[0];\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 1; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1)\n      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];\n  endgenerate\n\n  // 5. NORMAL PATH: left-shift prod by lz to align MSB at bit 21\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = prod_aligned[21:11];\n  wire              guard      = prod_aligned[10];\n  wire              sticky     = |prod_aligned[9:0];\n  wire              round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n\n  // Guard/sticky via explicit mux (22:1 mux for each)\n  // guard = prod[total_shift - 1] for total_shift 1..22, else 0\n  // sticky = below[total_shift - 2] for total_shift 2..23, else 0\n  reg guard_s_r, sticky_s_r;\n  always @* begin\n    guard_s_r = 1'b0;\n    sticky_s_r = 1'b0;\n    if (ts_ge0) begin\n      case (total_shift)\n        5'd1:  guard_s_r = prod[0];\n        5'd2:  begin guard_s_r = prod[1];  sticky_s_r = below[0];  end\n        5'd3:  begin guard_s_r = prod[2];  sticky_s_r = below[1];  end\n        5'd4:  begin guard_s_r = prod[3];  sticky_s_r = below[2];  end\n        5'd5:  begin guard_s_r = prod[4]; [ASSISTANT]
The case-based mux is worse (145502 vs 131196). The explicit case statement creates a large mux that's slower than the 32-bit indexed approach. V6 remains the best.

Let me try another approach: **reduce the `below` prefix-OR**. Currently it's a 22-deep ripple chain. Let me try a tree structure for faster computation. But actually, the `below` is only used for the subnormal sticky, which is not necessarily on the critical path.

Let me look at what I can do to reduce area. Let me try to **share logic between the normal and subnormal rounding paths**. Both compute a rounding addition:
- Normal: `{1'b0, mant_pre} + round_up` (12-bit add)
- Subnormal: `{1'b0, frac_trunc} + round_s` (11-bit add)

These are similar but different widths and inputs. Not easy to share.

Let me try to **simplify the LZC**. The `for` loop in the always block creates a priority encoder. Let me try a binary tree approach that might be more compact.

Actually, let me try a different strategy: **use the LZC from the `below` array**. The `below` array already has the prefix-OR. The LZC can be derived from `below`: `lz = 21 - (highest set bit of prod)`. The highest set bit is the complement of the highest zero in `below` (where `below[k] = 1` means bits 0..k have at least one 1). Actually, `below[k] = |prod[k:0]`, so `below[k] = 0` means all bits 0..k are 0, and `below[k] = 1` means at least one bit in 0..k is 1. The highest set bit of prod is at position `k` where `below[k] = 1` and `below[k+1]` either doesn't exist or... no, this doesn't work directly.

Actually, the MSB position is the highest k where prod[k] = 1. And `below[k] = |prod[k:0]`. So `below[21] = |prod[21:0] = |prod` (any bit set). If `prod = 0`, `below[21] = 0` and `lz = 22` (but original gives lz = 0 for prod=0). Wait, the original's loop sets `lz = 21 - i` when `prod[i]` is 1, but the default is `lz = 0`. So for `prod = 0`, `lz = 0` (no bits set, default). Hmm, but lz=0 means MSB at bit 21, which is wrong for prod=0. But prod=0 only happens when both inputs are zero, and the zero_in path handles this case.

Let me not change the LZC for now. Let me try something else.

Let me try **reducing the multiplier width**. The significands are 11-bit. Can I use a smaller multiplier? The product of two 11-bit numbers is 22 bits. I need all 22 bits for correctness. So no.

Let me try to **use a shared barrel shifter** for both paths. I'll make one shifter that does `prod << lz` (for normal) and `prod >> total_shift` (for subnormal). The shift amount would be `lz` (left) or `total_shift` (right), selected by `underflow`. But `underflow` depends on `carry` which depends on the normal path rounding which depends on the shift... circular.

However, I can compute `underflow` without `carry` by checking `lhs_no_carry <= limit_under`. The carry adds 1 to `lhs`. If `lhs_no_carry <= limit_under - 1`, then with carry it's `<= limit_under` → definitely underflow. If `lhs_no_carry > limit_under`, then with carry it's `> limit_under` → definitely not underflow. Only `lhs_no_carry == limit_under` is ambiguous (depends on carry). This is a narrow edge case.

For the barrel shifter, I could use the non-carry underflow to select the shift. But the edge case would need special handling.

Let me try another practical optimization: **reduce the width of the `prod_ext`/`below_ext` from 32 to 22 bits using Verilog's `inside` operator or a simple mask**. Actually, the 32-bit extension was what made v6 work well. Let me try 24 bits (just enough to handle index up to 23):

Actually, let me take a step back and think about what would really help. The v6 cost is 131196 (area=106.2, delay=1236). The original was 145429 (area=108.7, delay=1338). V6 improved both.

Let me try to further reduce the area by eliminating unnecessary logic. One idea: the `sub_is_zero` check requires `exp_sub == 0 & frac_sub == 0`. But when `carry_s = 0`, `exp_sub = 0` and `frac_sub = frac_sum[9:0]`. If `round_s = 0` and `frac_trunc = 0`, then `frac_sub = 0`. So `sub_is_zero = is_sub & ~carry_s & (frac_sub == 0)`. But this is what it already computes.

Let me try to **simplify the `ts_neg` computation**. Currently:
```verilog
wire [7:0] ts7 = 8'd26 - exp_sum;
wire ts_neg = (exp_sum > 8'd26);
```

Since `exp_sum = ea_eff + eb_eff` (each 5-bit), `exp_sum` ranges 0-62. `ts_neg` is true when `exp_sum > 26`, i.e., `exp_sum >= 27`. I can compute this as `exp_sum[5] | (exp_sum[4:0] >= 27)`... but 27 = 5'b11011, so `exp_sum >= 27` requires comparison. Actually, `exp_sum > 26` is the same as `exp_sum >= 27`. Since exp_sum is 8-bit, this comparison is not too expensive.

Let me try a completely different approach: **simplify by computing `total_shift` directly from the exponent bits**. `total_shift = 26 - exp_sum`. When `exp_sum >= 27`, `total_shift` is negative (no subnormal result). When `exp_sum = 0`, `total_shift = 26`. 

Actually, let me try to **merge `ts_neg` into the `ts_lt22` check**. Instead of separate `ts_neg` and `ts_lt22`, I can just check `ts7 < 22` (8-bit comparison). When `exp_sum > 26`, `ts7 = 26 - exp_sum` wraps to a large 8-bit value (e.g., 256 - 1 = 255), which is >= 22. So `ts7 < 22` handles both the negative case and the too-large case:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier v10\n// Simplified ts checks, merged ts_neg and ts_lt22.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  assign below[0] = prod[0];\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 1; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1)\n      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];\n  endgenerate\n\n  // 5. NORMAL PATH\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = prod_aligned[21:11];\n  wire              guard      = prod_aligned[10];\n  wire              sticky     = |prod_aligned[9:0];\n  wire              round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. SUBNORMAL PATH\n  wire [7:0] ts7         = 8'd26 - exp_sum;\n  wire       ts_lt22     = (ts7 < 8'd22);\n  wire [4:0] total_shift = ts7[4:0];\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] sub_shifted = ts_lt22 ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n\n  wire [31:0] prod_ext  = prod;\n  wire [31:0] below_ext = below;\n  wire [4:0]  ts_m1     = total_shift - 5'd1;\n  wire [4:0]  ts_m2     = total_shift - 5'd2;\n\n  // ts7 >= 1 means total_shift >= 1 (since ts7 < 22 for valid range)\n  // ts7 >= 2 means total_shift >= 2\n  wire guard_s  = (ts7 >= 8'd1) & prod_ext[ts_m1];\n  wire sticky_s = (ts7 >= 8'd2) & below_ext[ts_m2];\n\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v10.v')

[ASSISTANT]
V10 is worse (146279 vs 131196). The `ts7 >= 1` / `ts7 >= 2` checks are using the full 8-bit value, which includes the wrapped case. The issue is that when `ts7` wraps (exp_sum > 26), `ts7` becomes a large number like 255, and `ts7 >= 1` is true, but the guard index `prod_ext[ts_m1]` where `ts_m1 = 31` (from the wrapped total_shift) might access a nonzero bit. The `ts_lt22` check doesn't gate the guard/sticky anymore.

Wait, looking more carefully at v10: I removed the `ts_ge0` guard from `guard_s` and `sticky_s`. In v6, the guard was `ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 1) & prod_ext[ts_m1]`. In v10, it's just `(ts7 >= 1) & prod_ext[ts_m1]`. When `ts7` wraps (e.g., `ts7 = 255` when `exp_sum = 27`), `ts7 >= 1` is true, and `total_shift = ts7[4:0] = 31`, `ts_m1 = 30`, `prod_ext[30]` = 0 (since prod is 22-bit, zero-extended to 32). So guard_s = 0. That's actually correct.

But the 8-bit comparison `ts7 >= 1` vs the v6 approach of `ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 1)` might synthesize differently. The issue is that v10 uses `ts7` (8-bit) for the comparison while v6 uses `ts_ge0` (which is `~ts_neg` = `~(exp_sum > 26)`) and `total_shift` (5-bit). The different logic structure leads to different synthesis results.

Let me go back to v6 and try yet another optimization. Let me try to reduce the `below` prefix-OR to a tree structure:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier v11\n// Based on v6 with tree-structured below prefix-OR.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? [ASSISTANT]
V11 is worse (137249 vs 131196). The tree-structured `below` didn't help — the generate-based approach was actually better for synthesis. V6 remains the best.

Let me try to make v6 even better by looking at what specific logic is contributing to area and delay. Let me try a few targeted experiments:

1. **Remove `below[21]`** — it's `|prod` and only used when `total_shift = 23` via `below_ext[21]`. When `total_shift = 23`, guard is at position 22 which is out of range (0), so sticky doesn't matter. So I can safely remove it.

2. **Simplify the `sticky` OR reduction** in the normal path — `|prod_aligned[9:0]` is a 10-bit OR reduction.

Actually, the biggest opportunity might be to **reduce the number of bits in the `below` array** and the `prod_ext`/`below_ext` mux. Let me also try to **merge the `prod_ext` and `below_ext` into the main `prod` and `below`** by using a smarter indexing approach.

Let me try using `prod[total_shift - 1]` directly with a conditional that prevents out-of-bounds:
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- optimized IEEE binary16 multiplier v12\n// Based on v6, try to reduce prod_ext/below_ext from 32 to 22 bits\n// using a mask + shifted index approach.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (input  [15:0] a, input [15:0] b, output [15:0] y);\n\n  localparam BIAS = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E = 31;\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W = 22;\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  // leading-zero count\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  // below[k] = |prod[k:0] (prefix OR)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  assign below[0] = prod[0];\n  generate\n    for (genvar k = 1; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1)\n      assign below[k] = below[k-1] | prod[k];\n  endgenerate\n\n  // 5. NORMAL PATH\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod_aligned = prod << lz;\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = prod_aligned[21:11];\n  wire              guard      = prod_aligned[10];\n  wire              sticky     = |prod_aligned[9:0];\n  wire              round_up  = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. SUBNORMAL PATH\n  wire [7:0] ts7         = 8'd26 - exp_sum;\n  wire       ts_neg      = (exp_sum > 8'd26);\n  wire [4:0] total_shift = ts7[4:0];\n  wire       ts_ge0      = ~ts_neg;\n  wire       ts_lt22     = ts_ge0 & (total_shift < 5'd22);\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] sub_shifted = ts_lt22 ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n\n  // Guard/sticky: use bit-select with conditional\n  // When total_shift >= 1 and <= 22, guard = prod[total_shift-1]\n  // When total_shift >= 2 and <= 23, sticky = below[total_shift-2]\n  // For out-of-range, use 0. We achieve this by masking the index\n  // to 0-21 and using separate valid flags.\n  wire [4:0] ts_m1 = total_shift - 5'd1;\n  wire [4:0] ts_m2 = total_shift - 5'd2;\n  \n  // Use {1'b0, prod} and {1'b0, below} (23-bit) to handle index up to 22\n  wire [22:0] prod_pad  = {1'b0, prod};\n  wire [22:0] below_pad = {1'b0, below};\n  \n  wire guard_s  = ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 5'd1) & prod_pad[ts_m1];\n  wire sticky_s = ts_ge0 & (total_shift >= 5'd2) & below_pad[ts_m2];\n\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v12.v')

## Summary

### 1. Approaches Tried

Starting from `starting_point.v` (cost 145,429, area 108.7, delay 1338ps), I explored several optimization directions:

- **Normal-path left-shift alignment** (`design_v4.v` → 139,293; `design_v6.v` → **131,196**, the best result): Replaced the bidirectional shifter + variable `bit_at()` indexing in the normal path with a single left-shift by `lz` to align the MSB at bit 21, then extracted mantissa/guard/sticky from **fixed positions**. This eliminated the most expensive variable-indexing operations.

- **Merged subnormal path onto `prod_aligned`** (`design_v5.v`, 156,589): Tried routing both paths through the same left-shifted product. Correctness required careful handling of wrapping indices but added more logic than it saved.

- **Narrower exponent arithmetic** (`design_v7.v`/`v8.v`, 139,558): Dropped `exp_sum` from 8-bit to 6-bit. Synthesis picked a worse critical path through the shrunken comparators.

- **Explicit case-statement muxes** for guard/sticky (`design_v9.v`, 145,502): Replaced variable indexing with a 22-way `case`. Synthesis mapped it to a slower mux tree than the padded-index approach.

- **Tree-structured prefix-OR** for `below` (`design_v11.v`, 137,250) and 23-bit padding (`design_v12.v`): Both made synthesis slightly worse than the simple generate-based 22-bit chain with 32-bit zero-extension used in `v6`.

### 2. Most Impactful Optimizations

The single biggest win came from `design_v6.v` combining two changes:

1. **Normal path**: `prod_aligned = prod << lz` → fixed-position guard (`prod_aligned[10]`), sticky (`|prod_aligned[9:0]`), and mantissa (`prod_aligned[21:11]`). This removed the bidirectional barrel shifter, the `need_right`/`sr`/`sl` logic, and two `bit_at` function calls from the normal path.

2. **Subnormal path**: Replaced the `bit_at()` function (which used `integer` parameters and range checks) with 32-bit zero-extended `prod_ext`/`below_ext` indexed by 5-bit `ts_m1`/`ts_m2`, gated by `ts_ge0` and `total_shift >=` conditions. This let Yosys map the variable indexing to a cleaner mux structure.

Combined effect: area 108.7→106.2, delay 1338→1236ps, cost 145,429→**131,196** (a 10% improvement).

### 3. What Didn't Work / Caused Regressions

- **Unified `prod_aligned` for subnormal path** (`v5`): The extra `lz + total_shift` computation and a second prefix-OR (`below_a`) added more area than the sharing saved.
- **6/7-bit exponent arithmetic** (`v7`/`v8`): Narrower comparators picked a longer synthesis path; the 8-bit version was actually better optimized.
- **Case-statement guard/sticky mux** (`v9`): Synthesized to a deeper mux tree than the padded variable index.
- **23-bit or tree-structured `below`** (`v11`/`v12`): Synthesis handled the 32-bit zero-extension in `v6` more efficiently than smaller or restructured versions.
- **`target_delay` sweep** (300–2000ps): Had essentially no effect — the tool produced identical netlists across the range.

### 4. Lessons Learned

- **Function calls with `integer` parameters can inhibit synthesis**: Replacing `bit_at()` with explicit zero-padded wires + 5-bit indexing was a consistent win.
- **Fixed-position extraction beats variable indexing**: Aligning data to a known position and reading from constant bit ranges is cheaper than dynamic indexing, even though it requires an extra shift.
- **32-bit zero-extension is surprisingly efficient**: Yosys optimized the 32-bit padded wires better than 23-bit or clamped versions, likely because it can use the upper zero bits to simplify the mux logic.
- **Don't over-merge**: Attempting to unify the two shift+round paths (normal and subnormal) consistently added more overhead than it saved, because the circular dependency between `carry` and `underflow` forces parallel computation anyway.
- **RTL structure matters more than `target_delay`**: Sweeping the timing constraint had zero effect; the real lever was the structural description.

The best design is **`design_v6.v`** with cost **131,196** (area 106.2, delay 1235.5ps), a 10% improvement over the starting point.
